    sample, and interior judged by the dense versus the decimated argmax. **It is a harness
    difference and not an estimator one** — the ordering and sign of the margin are identical
    in all twelve cells measured — so nothing currently depends on it. It is recorded rather
    than rounded away because an unreconciled residual is how a real defect hides, and
    because M18 will implement exactly one of these conventions. **Flagged to the M36
    verifier.** Resolving it also fixes which convention `strehl-core` should use, which is a
    decision that has to be made anyway. `UNVERIFIED`.
    *(Raised by M35; numbered 85 to clear the reserved question blocks.)*

90. **What is the gate threshold, and does the built merge actually beat 1.0 on real
    frames?** `Gate::threshold` is the one free parameter in the merge and it is
    deliberately unmeasured and explicit at the API boundary rather than buried in a
    default. Its calibration target is already measured — **fact 212's `nwide = 3`** at full
    sampling density, the count of frames within 50% of peak height — and `Estimator::n_eff` is
    an output for exactly that purpose. The two acceptance sets are **not the same rule**
    (`nwide` thresholds on peak *height*, the gate on the calibrated *noise*), so 3 is a target
    to land near, not a number to reproduce. **Nothing here has touched a
    real frame yet.** The merge is proven never to be *noisier* than one frame, which is a
    statement about σ; fact 24 is the reason that is not sufficient, because a mean's σ
    can look harmless while its gradient collapses 58%. So the acceptance test is the
    **paired** one M2-verification established: flat-ROI noise ratio ≤ 1.0 *and* detail
    retained, measured with the same harness that produced 1.253×, on the same frames.
    Wiring the two-pass merge into `strehl-cli` as an additive subcommand — reusing the
    existing flat-ROI metric rather than reimplementing it, per discipline 210 — is the
    next build step. `UNVERIFIED`.
    *(Raised by M37; numbered 90 to clear the reserved question blocks.)*

    > **M38: half answered, half refused.** Both acceptance tests **pass on real frames**
    > (facts 320, 322) — flat-ROI 0.890x at gate 2.0 against argmax's 1.253x, detail retained
    > to −4.6% once the noise confound is removed. But the question's *premise* is wrong:
    > **there is no single threshold** (fact 324). At gate 2.0 `n_eff` spans 2.26–7.42 across
    > five stacks, and the threshold that lands on fact 212's target of 3 ranges 1.1–2.9. The
    > free parameter should be **the target `n_eff`, not the gate** — which is a better API
    > anyway, since it is the quantity with a measured calibration behind it and the one a
    > user can reason about. What remains open is how the solve is specified; see question 95.

95. **What is the right gate parameterisation, now that a fixed threshold is known not to
    transfer?** Fact 324 measured `n_eff/N` clustering at 0.068–0.079 on four of five stacks,
    which says the acceptance set is roughly a fixed *fraction* of the bracket — the signature
    of a through-focus peak of fixed optical width sampled at varying density. Three candidates,
    all cheap because pass two folds many accumulators for the price of one decode:
    **(a)** solve per stack for a target `n_eff` by bisection on the gate;
    **(b)** normalise the gate by the measured focus-step density, which makes it a statement
    about depth rather than about frame count;
    **(c)** normalise per pixel by the local through-focus curve width, which is the only one
    that adapts *within* an image and is the natural precursor to the posterior formulation.
    `109MSDCF`'s 0.131 outlier must be explained before any of them is trusted — it is the
    shallowest stack at 22 frames, so a boundary or under-sampling effect is the first
    hypothesis. **Also unresolved:** `at_bracket_boundary` reads 7.60% on `coffee-bean` and
    2.02% on `119MSDCF`, while fact 300 reports 7.38% for `119MSDCF`. The definitions differ
    (all-pixel regularised winner index against a parabola-fit peak on a reliable subset), so
    this is not yet a conflict — but the numbers are close enough, and attached to the wrong
    stacks, that one of the two should be re-derived before either is cited. `UNVERIFIED`.
    *(Raised by M38.)*

    > **Boundary discrepancy resolved by reading M35's harness, not by re-measuring.**
    > `experiments/m35-fit-generality/src/01-build.py:70` computes
    > `((am <= 1) | (am >= n - 2)).mean()` — **two frames deep at each end**, on a
    > **decimated** stack, from an **unregularised** argmax. `at_bracket_boundary` is **one**
    > frame deep, on the **full** stack, from the **regularised** winner. All three differences
    > push M35's number up, so 7.38% and 2.02% are the same reality read two ways.
    > **The confirmation is that both stacks scale by the same factor**: 7.38/2.02 = **3.65**
    > on `119MSDCF` against 2.08/0.61 = **3.41** on `112MSDCF`, agreeing to 7%. If either
    > number were simply wrong there would be no reason for the ratios to agree. `coffee-bean`
    > at 7.60% is genuinely the worst-covered stack in the corpus on the stricter definition —
    > the apparent match with 119MSDCF's 7.38% was coincidence. **`merge.rs`'s doc comment
    > invited exactly this false comparison and has been corrected.** The remaining part of
    > this question — the gate parameterisation and `109MSDCF`'s 0.131 outlier — stands.

    > **Candidate (a) is built and confirmed, and it exposes the next question.**
    > `--target-n-eff` solves the ladder by log-log interpolation: 2.0964 on `109MSDCF` for a
    > target of 3, which measures **2.989** when re-run — 0.37% (fact 330). `coffee-bean` needs
    > **1.1063** for the same target, so the parameterisation transfers where the gate does not.
    > **But fact 331 undercuts the control variable itself**: `coffee-bean` holds 62–77% of
    > pixels at `n_eff = 1` while its *mean* reaches 7.42, so a mean target buys a lot of
    > averaging for a minority of pixels and none for most of the image. **The open question is
    > therefore no longer "gate or n_eff" but which statistic of the `n_eff` distribution to
    > target** — the mean, a percentile, or the `n_eff = 1` share. Candidate (c), normalising
    > per pixel by the local through-focus width, is the one that dissolves the question rather
    > than answering it, and it is the same object the posterior formulation needs anyway.

    > **One weak lead, recorded so it is not re-derived.** If `n_eff` tracks the peak width in
    > frames, the natural normaliser is the bracket span *actually used* rather than the frame
    > count. M35's `01-build.json` has the argmax percentiles for two stacks: `119MSDCF` spans
    > p05–p95 = 4–30 (26 frames of 33), `112MSDCF` spans 11–66 (55 of 79). Normalising gate
    > 2.0's `n_eff` by that span gives **0.0868 and 0.1132** — a 1.30× spread, **worse** than
    > `n_eff/N`'s 0.0684 and 0.0788 (1.15×). So the obvious refinement makes the fit worse on
    > the only two stacks where the data already exists, which is evidence against it but is
    > **two points and does not include the `109MSDCF` outlier itself** — the stack that raised
    > the question is not in M35's build. Getting it needs argmax percentiles from the merge
    > command, roughly ten lines and five runs. Not started, so that the night ends on a
    > verified boundary rather than mid-measurement.

100. **Which argmax baseline is this pipeline's, given that fact 19's 1.253× does not
     reproduce on the shipped operator?** M39 measured **1.0678× unregularised** and
     **1.0739× regularised** on the same frames and ROI where M2 measured 1.253× and 1.075×.
     The regularised halves agree to 0.1%; the unregularised ones do not, and the gap cannot be
     the regulariser, because here regularisation moves the ratio the *wrong way* (up 0.6%,
     not down 14%). The likely cause is the focus operator — M2's was a ring difference, the
     shipped one is a normalised Tenengrad — which would mean **the two facts describe
     different selectors and only one of them is the product's**. Three things follow, in
     descending confidence: **(a)** the merge's win must be quoted same-operator, 0.890×
     against 1.0739×, a **17%** improvement (fact 343); **(b)** fact 19's "the defect the
     project exists to remove is measured on our own files" remains true but is measured on an
     operator the project does not ship, so its *magnitude* is not this pipeline's; **(c)** if
     the shipped Tenengrad is only 1.07× noisier under selection where the ring operator was
     1.25×, then **the operator swap already bought most of what fact 19 priced**, and the
     estimation merge's remaining margin over good selection is thinner than the corpus
     implies. **(c) is the one to test first, and it is cheap**: run both operators through the
     same harness on the same ROI. It moves the headline number the whole thesis is sold on,
     so nothing downstream should be re-argued until it is settled. `UNVERIFIED`.
     *(Raised by M39.)*
     **ANSWERED by M17.** The baseline is neither the ring nor plain Tenengrad: it is **`tenengrad-sq-w13`**, `box_mean₂₇(gx²+gy²)/σ²`. It takes the monotone-edge blind spot from **D = 1.0000 to 0.1860**, is quality-neutral on M13's occluding-edge cells (15/30, p = 1.00), noise-neutral (ΔN = −0.0015), and **cheaper than the shipped operator (0.96×)** because it drops a per-pixel `sqrt`. It is now the operator in `crates/strehl-core/src/focus.rs`, with the ring retained only as the comparison the tests assert against. **Caveat carried forward:** `w13` was chosen by M13 for the *mean* operator and candidate (c)'s per-shape sign is not uniform, which is consistent with a window mismatch — the ladder has not been re-run for `tenengrad-sq`. See owner question in `docs/research/OWNER-QUESTIONS.md`.
80. **Which definition of "predicted sigma" should M18 ship, given that the gated and ungated
    forms have opposite signs against sub-frame deficit?** The gated form carries acceptance-set
    information; the ungated form is what a user would expect from the name. They are different
    channels and should probably both exist, or the gated one should be renamed
81. **Should the acceptance-set size ship as a fourth real-valued channel?** It is the only
    quantity in this study with a consistent worst-5% lift across all three stacks (1.94x / 2.67x
    / 2.36x), and it is already computed as a by-product of C1
82. **Does C3's specificity collapse on 119MSDCF and 112MSDCF because those stacks are coarsely
    sampled and dim, or because +0.5560 is a coffee-bean artifact?** Separating the two needs a
    decimated coffee-bean arm matched to 119MSDCF's effective focus step
83. **Should M32's headline sigma count be restated at 84–512, and does fact 264's reconciliation
    with M20 survive at the lower figure?** It should — 84 sigma is still overwhelming — but fact
    264 quotes "10^2–10^3 sigma" and the honest range is now 10^2 at the low end and barely
    5x10^2 at the high end
84. **Is the dense 105-frame grid adequate?** The pre-registered bar was 3x; the like-for-like
25. **Does the sub-pixel locking artefact of fact 173 also bias the *unplanted* edge measurements
     the pipeline actually uses?** The `1/S` conservation defect is measured with no resampler in
     the path, so it is not the same artefact — but `coffee-bean`'s span-1 edges have a measured
     displacement of ~0.19 px, squarely in the locking zone, while its long edges do not. If locking
     contributes to fact 43's span dependence, the fix is a **phase dither or an upsampled
     correlation peak, not a calibration**. *Testable:* re-measure `coffee-bean` span-1 edges with
     the moving frame pre-shifted by a known integer-plus-half-pixel offset that is then removed
     analytically, and see whether `1/S_48(1)` moves off 3.26. **This is the live thread M23 leaves
     behind, and it is cheap.**
26. **Is R5 (blur-equalised edges) now the better candidate?** It attacks the same defect without
     planting anything, so it is immune to the mechanism that broke R4. Untested.
101. **What should a strictly detail-free region read under the corrected detail metric, and does
     the flat ROI qualify?** Fact 430 measures −11.5% where 1.0× is the naive expectation, but the
     ROI's single-frame corrected reading is 14.7270 rather than 0, so it is low-detail, not
     no-detail. Until this is settled the gate-0 denominator is not trustworthy and M38's retention
     band cannot be quoted. **Testable, and cheap:** synthesise a genuinely flat plane at the ROI's
     mean level with the calibrated noise model, run the same metric, and read the answer directly —
     the simulator (M25) exists to make exactly this kind of question decidable. Then re-derive the
     retention band against whichever denominator survives.
    measurement is 3.24x and the calibration-corrected one ~2.7–2.8x. If the ground truth is only
    ~3x better than the thing it judges, how much of the 2.876% "true deficit" is the ground
    truth's own error?

**Licensing, for the record.** MIT OR Apache-2.0 on our own code is standard and fine. The
practical obligation comes from linking LGPL-2.1 `rawler`: **anyone we distribute a binary to must
be able to relink it against a modified `rawler`.** Ship it dynamically linked, or publish object
files / a reproducible build. Statically linking it into a monolithic binary and shipping only that
binary is the trap. Separately, the "link but do not read" constraint on GPL sources (enfuse,
`align_image_stack`, Hugin, darktable, RawTherapee) is a constraint on *how the project works* and
is a written project rule in `AGENTS.md`.
102. **What is the realistic spread of `k` across focus measures anyone would
    actually contribute?** Fact 437 prices the damage using `k` = 5.94, taken
    from two operators already in this repository, but that is an illustration
    and not a distribution. If plausible contributed operators cluster near
    `k` = 1, the seam is a documentation problem; if they spread as widely as
    the two built-ins do, the load-time refusal in fact 438 is mandatory. Cheap
    to answer: measure the noise SD of the M11/M14/M17 bake-off arms, which are
    already implemented, and read the spread directly.

    **ANSWERED by fact 439** (M43 step 2b): `k` spans 0.644 to 6.680 across
    seven textbook operators, a 10.4x spread, with the two canonical
    shape-from-focus measures at the top. The spread is as wide as the
    built-ins', so the load-time refusal is mandatory. The same work found
    that fact 437's `k` = 5.94 was itself wrong — see fact 500.

103. **Does any focus operator close the smooth-ramp blind spot?** `tenengrad-sq` takes discrimination D from 1.0000 to 0.6802 on a width-9 ramp
    and **no radius in the w8-w40 ladder improves it** (fact 487). Ramps are the common macro case — specular falloff on a carapace, defocused
    backgrounds, petal edges — and fact 73's original argument called them out by name. Half a cure on the most common case may be the largest
    remaining defect in the focus operator.

104. **Is M13's planted-edge regret curve the same shape as a natural depth step's?** The entire `WINDOW_RADIUS` lineage — M13, M15, M17, M42 —
    inherits **one plant**, and not one of those four milestones has tested whether its regret curve resembles a real occluding edge's.
    M42 lists this first under what it could not measure. If the shapes differ, every radius number in the corpus moves together.

105. **Should worst-case regret be weighted, and how?** Mean regret selects w11; worst-case regret selects w9, and w13's tail penalty is **51%**
    (19.83 DN vs 13.14 DN, fact 485). M42 explicitly declined to make this call unilaterally. It matters most once per-pixel confidence is
    calibrated on tail behaviour, since a tail-weighted objective would also change what the confidence map is promising.

106. **Does the C1 mask move at a wider confidence tail (25-50%)**, where the boundary crosses into n >= 2 pixels? M40 measured only the shipped 5% tail, where fact 450 shows the mask is
    completely invariant. If the mask is invariant at every tail width, the C1 channel cannot be validated by any experiment of this shape and needs a different instrument.

107. **What is the selection inflation on REAL data as a function of n?** The naive stratification confounds n with scene flatness — pixels with large n are pixels where many frames agreed, which is
    also where the scene is flat. A matched-content stratification is needed before any real-data curve can be read as a selection effect.

108. **Does fixed-pattern noise lift the true inflation above the pure-noise plant's ceiling of 1.045?** PRNU, dark current and M16's orientation FPN are all absent from M40's plant. If any of them
    raises the winner's curse, then fact 446's ceiling — currently the strongest single argument against 1.074 — is not a bound. This is cheap to test once the flat-field campaign lands.

109. **Should `SELECTION_INFLATION` be two constants, a per-n curve, or nothing at all?** Fact 447 gives a stable pixel/gradient split (~1.045 and ~1.031); fact 448 gives a smooth curve in n.
    A fourth option M40 raises is that the detail metric should stop using a model noise term entirely and use a **paired difference of two independent merges** instead, which needs no constant.

110. **Should `report_flat_roi`'s banner still quote fact 19's 1.253x?** Neither M39 nor M40 can reproduce it and the operator that produced it is retired, yet it is printed live to users.

111. **What accounts for the 0.03-0.05 merged-noise floor deficit (fact 542)?** PRNU is the leading suspect and is unmeasured. The flat-field capture already listed in `docs/captures-needed.md` would settle it. Until then the twin's absolute noise levels are not quotable.

112. **Would a real-texture latent scene — a high-resolution photograph of an organic subject used as `L` — close the gate 1c gap?** The simulator supports it; the corpus does not yet contain a suitable high-magnification flat capture.

113. **Why does the fitted `coc_px_per_frame` run to the grid edge on `coffee-bean` (12.0 px/frame, RMS shape error 0.17) but sit comfortably at 1.5 on 109MSDCF?** Is real defocus growth non-linear in frame index, or is the subject's depth range doing the work? Bears directly on whether the focus axis can be treated as uniformly sampled.

114. **What gate would be sensitive to the PSF?** Fact 544 says none of the current ones are. Candidates M25 names: through-focus MTF against M5's measured aperture sweep; the through-focus curve *shape* (real curves are asymmetric and fall faster than any simulated one at `coffee-bean`); edge-spread reconstruction against a real razor edge. **This question gates every optical use of the simulator.**

115. **Does the excess deficit at wide support (`ring-4-12`: 0.022 vs 0.115) share a cause with the floor deficit of fact 542, or is it a separate mismatch in how correlated the real focus field is at long range?**

116. **Is the simulated speckle excess (50.3% vs 38.2%) fully explained by the subject-spectrum mismatch of fact 546, or does it survive spectrum shaping?**

117. **Does arm D′ — guarded RIF without the diffraction term — beat the shipped merge on a metric with power, at ≤ 1.10× its noise?** It is the only survivor here and it was never what R3 asked for. (M44 §8.)

118. **What ground-truth-free detail metric *does* have power on this sensor, given that split-half FRC does not?** A candidate: FRC between the merges of two *independent captures* of the same subject (112MSDCF / 113MSDCF), where the fixed-pattern component is still common-mode but the read-noise realisation and the subject sampling are not — this would bound how much of §3.6's effect is FPN and how much is amplitude-free correlation. §3.8.3 puts a number on the prize: recovery scales as (1 − ρ), and the measured common-mode fraction on one capture is 0.63–0.98. (M44 §8.) **REOPENED by facts 695-706**: fact 619 answered this and is refuted. Fact 708 narrows it usefully -- an oracle at one frequency is equally blind, so the answer cannot be a single-frequency statistic of any kind. See question 164 and milestone M57.

119. **Is there any admissible detail metric here at all?** The band-power ratio and FRC-at-`f_sig` both recover a planted blur at ≥ +0.99, but both also fire at ≥ +0.94 on a flat ROI, so neither separates "blurred the scene" from "smoothed the noise". Does a *paired* statistic — detail reading conditioned on the flat reading of the same merge — separate them, and can that be shown on a plant where only one of the two is present? (M44 §8.) **REOPENED, and it is now the project's largest blocker again.** Fact 619 closed this; facts 695-706 refute fact 619. The paired statistic this question proposes was what `sigpow` became, and it failed for reasons this question did not anticipate: non-monotonicity in true resolution, gain sensitivity, and a nuisance term with 50x the leverage of the signal.

120. **Why is the `sb = 0` split-half FRC reference censored on every source, including the analytic truth channel?** Correlated content surviving to Nyquist (aliasing, PRNU) and spectral leakage from the window are both candidates; a Blackman window and a 9×9 high-pass pre-filter each restore an uncensored crossing at `sb = 4` but neither was traced to the `sb = 0` case here. (M44 §8.)

121. **Does the diffraction term become safe once the merge aligns?** All six arms here ran unaligned; misregistration puts energy above `f_sig` that the deconvolution then amplifies. (M44 §8.)

122. **What is arm D's out-of-range fraction across the whole corpus, not one ROI per stack?** M20's 15.1 % headline was one capture from a 3.5–65.8 % range. (M44 §8.)

123. **Should the merge adopt the pre-normalised field or the measured-constant field?** Pre-normalising makes `--gate` dimensionless and therefore portable across operators — §F5.3's point 3 argues it is the only version in which a shared preset means anything — at the cost of changing ~7e-7 of admission decisions. The measured-constant form keeps the arithmetic shape byte-for-byte and changes no decision, but leaves `--gate` operator-dependent, so a preset tuned on tenengrad is silently 6.6x off on SML. **Both are implemented in `strehl-plugin`.** This is a product decision about what a gate number *means* to a user, not a technical one.

124. **Should the pending `tenengrad-sq` + r11 change re-derive `FOCUS_NOISE_SD` through the calibrator instead of hard-coding a new literal?** Facts 509-511 price that change at `k` = 1.528. Routing it through `strehl-plugin` would be the calibrator's first real use and would **retire the compiled-in constant permanently**, which is the whole point of fault 5. The alternative is a third hand-measured literal in a file that has now had two of them go wrong.

125. **Is a runtime panic acceptable at plugin registration?** `Window::Custom` and `Normalisation::Custom` can only be enforced by panic, because Rust cannot require that a *provided* trait method be overridden when an associated value takes a particular variant. The alternative is splitting the trait so that a custom-window operator is a different type and the invalid state does not exist. The panic fires at registration, not mid-merge, which is the least-bad time for it.

126. **Where does this body's gain actually stop tracking ISO?** Fact 550
establishes `g` is wrong at ISO 50 and right at ISO 100 on two independent
routes, but does **not** locate the transition: the corpus holds no ISO 64 or
ISO 80 capture, so `max(ISO, 100)` is the simplest rule consistent with the
evidence, not a measured one. Two frames at ISO 64 and 80 settle it — requested
as `captures-needed.md` section 10.

127. **How much of fact 548's 2.1x multi-peak over-production is the 7%
structure deficit of fact 551?** The two are linked by argument, not
measurement. A probe that scales latent contrast alone, holding spectrum and
noise fixed, answers it in one render — and if contrast does not explain it,
the noise model at ISO 50 is still wrong somewhere the dark frames cannot see.

128. **Which statistic is the noise model supposed to predict — the pooled RMS,
the mean pixel, or the median pixel?** They differ by 18% at ISO 100 and 32% at
ISO 1600 (fact 556), and every sensor constant currently uses the RMS by
accident rather than by choice. The gate is per-pixel, and hot pixels are
handled elsewhere (M22, M29), which argues for the typical pixel; conservatism
argues for the RMS. The honest answer may be that a scalar is the wrong shape
and the model owes a per-pixel sigma *distribution*. **M48 is deciding this and
must write the decision where the constants live.**

129. **Is there a constant additive DN-domain noise floor the model omits?**
**ARGUED, from a fit I have not validated.** `sigma_DN^2 = a*ISO^2 + c` fitted
on the verified table, anchored on each mode's endpoints, leaves residuals
within **±1%** across both modes against **±4%** for the best single `RN_e` —
and the two independently-fitted floors, 0.470 DN (high) and 0.513 DN (low),
agree to 9%, consistent with one floor near 0.49 DN. 14-bit quantisation alone
would give 0.289 DN. If real, `sigma^2 = g*S + (RN_e*g)^2` is the wrong shape at
low signal, which is exactly where the gate does its work. **This is a
hypothesis formed from an endpoint-anchored two-point fit, which is the weakest
kind; M48 is trying to kill it with a proper all-ISO fit.** Do not build on it
until that lands.
130. **What causes the residual ~5% gradient anisotropy, and does it matter on scene content?** It is real (11/11 ISOs), it is **not** banding (two-thirds survives row/column mean removal), and it is unexplained. **ARGUED:** column-parallel shared-ADC readout has the right sign, and M4's unresolved G2/B plane excess may be the same effect. The downstream half is largely answered -- M16 measured the effect on the window statistic at 0.0 windows -- so this is a sensor-characterisation question, not a blocker. `UNVERIFIED`.
131. **Why is M50's total fixed pattern 1.4-1.6% above M4 §5 at ISO 1600-2000 only?** Candidates: M4's bias correction over-subtracting where `sigma_temporal` is large, or a weak frame-to-frame temporal correlation that cross-half covariance retains and M4's estimator removes. **The second would matter elsewhere** -- it would mean averaged frames denoise slightly worse than the model predicts at high ISO. Cheap to settle. `UNVERIFIED`.
132. **What plant would actually contain the occlusion-veiling effect?** The simulator produces enrichment 0.23x where the real capture gives 4.38x, and it is not the defocus rate (flat across 12x), not the occluder luminance step (0.24x to 0.28x), and not the gain (stable across a 2x error). **ARGUED:** the single-disc occluder is too simple and real veiling needs multi-scale occluding structure. **Until this is answered the simulator is barred from scoring occlusion**, alongside its existing bar on scoring deconvolution (fact 549). `UNVERIFIED`.

133. **Who owns `read_noise_e_low`'s shape error?** -2.3 % at ISO 100 but **+10.4 % at ISO 200 and +11.4 % at ISO 250**, and fact 557 says no scalar can fix it. M48 was tasked with `read_noise_e_high` alone. The corpus has no ISO 200/250 stack, so nothing measurable is wrong with today's output -- which is exactly the condition under which fact 579 says a finding sits unowned indefinitely. Either name an owner or write down that it is deliberately deferred and why. `UNVERIFIED`.

134. **Should the clip mask reach the focus volume before any further merge claim is published?** Fact 203's consequence -- mask at/above container max *before* the focus volume -- is unbuilt, and fact 577 measures the cost: always-clipped pixels read cross-frame focus contrast at its **maximum, 1.0000**, while carrying zero information. It is zero-population on all four headline stacks and 1.26 % / 62,054 pixels on the two coffee stacks. **ARGUED:** a defect that is exactly zero on every stack used for headlines and non-zero on the two that are not is the most dangerous population profile a defect can have. `UNVERIFIED`.

135. **Is `--target-n-eff` a product feature or a diagnostic?** Fact 324 says the gate threshold must be solved per stack; fact 582 says the solver cannot produce an image, because `--out` requires exactly one `--gate`. Either wire the solve to the output path -- in which case the shipped default becomes a target n_eff rather than a threshold, which is a user-facing design change -- or state that the per-stack solve is a diagnostic and that shipped images use a fixed non-transferable gate. It is currently neither, and the CLI reads as though it were the former. `UNVERIFIED`.

136. **What made a recorded anomaly survive twelve milestones?** Every ISO-50 result was reachable from a two-line check -- dark sigma at ISO 50 against ISO 100 -- which the corpus had **already performed and recorded** at M4 §8.2, and §8.3 had already called the implied 8.6189 e- "visibly nonsense ... the tell". The anomaly was written down, correctly interpreted, and never escalated to the constant that produced it. **ARGUED:** this is the same shape as fact 589 -- the correction was written into the report instead of into the value -- and it is the strongest argument in the corpus for a mechanism that links a measured anomaly to the artifact it indicts. `UNVERIFIED`.

137. **Is the real 4.38x driven by depth *multiplicity* rather than alpha veiling at
     all?** M25's plant is two discrete layers; a real macro scene is a continuous depth
     field, so a detector window on a real capture sees many depths present at once
     rather than one foreground veiling one background. If the real signal is depth
     multiplicity, then the plant was built for the wrong phenomenon and no amount of
     occluder tuning will reach it -- which would explain fact 603's asymptote. This is
     the only surviving candidate M25 can name after geometry, defocus rate and
     luminance step have all been eliminated by measurement. **A milestone, not a
     probe.** (Facts 568, 603.)

138. **Is the real 4.38x partly M21's own false-positive rate?** Fact 605 measures the
     estimator pinned at 0.98-1.06x across a 57x range of synthetic geometry while the
     oracle beneath it moves 4.5x; fact 567 measures it over-flagging true depth steps by
     3.5-5.7x at 1.8-3.4% precision. Nobody has asked whether the 4.38x it reports on
     `coffee-bean` is an occlusion signal or an artifact of that error rate. **This has
     never been tested and it invalidates any occlusion work keyed on this estimator if
     it goes the wrong way.** Note the honest difficulty: real captures have no truth
     map, which is why the plant existed -- so answering this needs either a plant that
     passes fact 568's bar or a different, truth-bearing route entirely.

139. **Wiring `sigpow_fsig` into the CLI means merging two disjoint half-stacks on every
     run -- roughly 2x the merge cost. Default, `--verify` flag, or not at all?**
     **FOR THE OWNER.** This is the price of the tool being able to certify its own
     central claim (fact 619). My recommendation is `--verify`, defaulting off, because
     the GPU path's single-digit-second merge is a headline number and doubling it by
     default trades the wrong thing -- but this is a product decision, not a technical
     one, and it is yours. **MOOT: there is nothing to wire.** Facts 695-706 refute the metric. Fact 644's measured 0.4% cost stands and applies to any replacement with the same two-half-merge shape.

140. **Should the shipped `mean_grad` detail invariant be replaced, or kept alongside
     `sigpow` as a cheap screen with its limitation documented?** Fact 624 measures it at
     0.233 separation and 0.018 paired; fact 145 already established it is one-sided.
     There is a real argument for keeping it -- it is nearly free and it does catch
     collapse -- but only if what it cannot do is printed next to what it says.

141. **With a resolution statistic finally available, is re-pricing selection a milestone
     worth opening?** `merge.rs` now sets `SELECTION_INFLATION = 1.0`, which leaves `n=1`
     unpriced and the noise model deliberately optimistic (fact 458 and the M40 fold).
     That was the right call while the penalty was unmeasurable. Fact 619 may make it
     measurable.

142. **Can the FPN fraction be measured independently rather than solved from an FRC
     reading?** Fact 610's rho = 0.3094 comes from one flat ROI on one body. The owner has
     delivered **242 dark frames across 11 ISOs** at `D:\macro-dark-frames`, which is
     plausibly enough to measure rho directly -- and an independent route to a number
     currently obtained by inversion would be worth more than the number was. Nobody has
     tried.
     **ANSWERED by fact 663, by refusing the premise: it cannot be measured from darks. A dark frame has no photon shot noise in its denominator and no PRNU in its numerator, so `rho_dark` is a different quantity that coincides with `rho_M46` only as `S -> 0`. `rho` is a property of a sensor AT A SIGNAL LEVEL (discipline 329). Facts 670-672 bound the DSNU contribution at 0.12% of what 0.3094 requires, robustly across three decades of assumed signal; question 155 names the flat-field capture that could measure the term that actually carries it.**

143. **Should invariants 4 and 5 be restated on the delivered image rather than the focus
     field?** Fact 623 notes they prove the regulariser's behaviour on the focus *field*,
     while the thesis is claimed about the delivered image. That is a weaker claim than
     the one made in public, and fact 619 removes the excuse for it.
144. **What gate does `foam` actually want?** It reaches `n_eff` = 3 only at gate **21.99**, 5x higher than any other stack and above the entire shipped ladder. `foam` is ISO 1000 with 1.20% of pixels clipped in at least one frame and a `boundary %` of exactly 0.00. Is this a real property of a low-contrast subject, or is the focus field dominated by something the noise model mis-prices? (M52, fact 628)
145. **Should the host calibrate at two sigmas rather than one?** One extra plane at, say, sigma = 16 would make a mis-declared `Normalisation` degree detectable at load time, turning the seam's last remaining declaration into a measurement. Is there a reason not to? (M52, fact 633)
146. **Is a per-operator noise constant worth its complexity, if the gate must be solved per (operator, stack) pair regardless?** The constant buys a meaningful *unit* for `--gate`; fact 630 shows it does not buy transferability. Fact 438's load-time refusal may be defensible purely as a unit check rather than as a calibration. (M52)
147. **Does the pending M17+M42 edit land in `focus.rs` or through the plugin `FocusMeasure` path?** Fact 509's 1.53x is right for one and wrong for the other by roughly the local sigma, and **no document in the repository says which**. This blocks change (c). (M52, fact 632) **ANSWERED by fact 662: it lands in `focus.rs`, because `strehl-cli` does not depend on `strehl-plugin` at all (fact 583) and the binary has no other path. So `k = 1.5306` is the wrong correction for the code that would actually change, and change (c) is not to be applied as specified.**
148. **Does the shipped pipeline guarantee a stable depth ordering at the point where a half-stack split would be taken**, including after speckle and bracket-boundary rejection? The split contract requires parity *on the depth-sorted index*, and fact 641 measures that getting this wrong returns 0.47% of the true value while still looking like a reading. (M46 App.A)
149. **Should `sigpow` read the same ROI the existing flat/detail acceptance invariants use, a new one, or should the CLI read several and report the worst?** It is defined on one square ROI, and fact 650's `d` thresholds are one ROI's calibration rather than a constant. (M46 App.A) **MOOT** per facts 695-706, but the question is well-posed and should be re-asked of any replacement: fact 650's thresholds were one ROI's calibration rather than a constant, and fact 707 shows an unidentified mechanism operates on flat ROIs specifically.
150. **At 0.4% marginal cost (fact 644), should verification be the default with `--no-verify` as the escape, rather than an opt-in `--verify`?** This reverses the premise of question 139, which assumed roughly 2x. (M46 App.A) **WITHDRAWN by facts 695-706: the metric this question would have defaulted on is non-monotone in true resolution, its decision threshold is saturated by a 0.15% gain change, and it reads negative in 7 of 36 real cells. The 0.4% cost figure stands and is now moot.**
151. **Should `FOCUS_NOISE_SD`'s doc comment state that it is sensor-independent?** One sentence, in `merge.rs`, and it is the durable fix for the false premise that commissioned an entire milestone. Owned by whoever holds `merge.rs`. (M48, fact 653)
152. **Should the four `1.47` fixtures become `plane(0.229, 4.20)`?** Zero behavioural effect is expected at every site and measured at the one that could have been masked, but they currently describe a sensor that does not exist. Spans `focus.rs`, `noise.rs`, `measure.rs` and an example -- three different owners. (M48, fact 659)
153. **Is there any real capture where the read-noise error bites?** It exceeds 5% only below ~10 DN black-subtracted at ISO 1000, and no frame in the corpus goes near it (0.1st percentile = 132 DN). A genuinely dark subject at high ISO could; worth re-checking if the corpus ever gains one. (M48)
154. **Does the per-pixel `r(S)` variation across a frame justify a spatially-varying gate calibration?** `normalise_by_luma_sigma` divides per pixel, so the ratio varies within a frame as well as across ISO; measured range is small (p99 0.482%). The machinery is the same as question 130's and the two should be scoped together if either is built. (M48)

155. **Can PRNU be measured on this body at all?** It is the term that actually carries M46's `rho`, and darks cannot see it. The standard method is a flat-field: two exposures of a uniformly lit surface at different levels, differenced to remove FPN and ratioed to isolate it. **This is a small capture request** -- a few frames of an evenly lit blank card at two exposures, ISO 500. (M48b, fact 671)
156. **Is M46's `rho` contaminated by residual scene structure?** A cheap discriminator exists with no new captures: FRC between two captures of *different* subjects at the same ISO. Sensor FPN is common to those; scene structure is not. **If the correlation survives a subject change it is the sensor; if it collapses, part of 0.3094 is the coffee.** (M48b, fact 671)
157. **Is the ~0.4 DN DSNU floor the same physical thing as M48's ~0.51 DN temporal floor?** They are measured in orthogonal domains and cannot be the same statistic, but could share a cause -- an ADC or reference term contributing both a per-read and a per-pixel spread. Both sit near 14-bit quantisation's 0.289 DN. **Do not conflate them in the meantime.** (M48b)
158. **Is the ~14% spectral roll-off toward Nyquist real inter-pixel crosstalk?** Systematic across all 11 ISOs. Worth naming only if something downstream divides by FPN power at high frequency. (M48b, fact 675)
159. **Does applying the M22 hot-pixel map first change any FRC or censoring result that currently attributes its behaviour to FPN?** Fact 666 measures hot pixels at ~46% of the ISO-1600 fixed pattern. Cheap to test, and it bears directly on M46's censoring conclusions. (M48b, fact 666)
160. **Should censored contributors be excluded from the gated mean?** `has_censored_contributor` fires on 63,343 samples at gate 4.0 on `coffee-bean` (0.19%), where the mean is biased *upward* by a pinned non-winner. Excluding them needs a per-plane admitted count -- 4x `Vec<u16>`, **+66 MB at full resolution**. That is a memory decision for the orchestrator, not a local one; the flag is exposed so the cost is measurable rather than hypothetical. (M54)
161. **Which decoder's active area is correct, `rawler`'s 3516x2344 or `rawpy`'s 3514x2344?** Fact 691. Every cross-checked percentage in this corpus carries a 0.057% systematic until this is settled, and the analysis scripts and the shipped pipeline currently disagree. **The actionable half, which the fold dropped: every percentage M54 published uses the `rawler` count, which is what the shipped pipeline sees** -- so M54's numbers are internally consistent with the code and inconsistent with the analysis scripts, not floating between them. (M54 s9, fact 691)
162. **What should the noise model report above 14848 DN?** Fact 692 measures the merge emitting up to 15871. Options: extrapolate and say so, clamp, or emit a censored-confidence flag and refuse a number. This interacts with question 160 and with whatever confidence semantics get defined. (M54, fact 692)
163. **Should the contaminated-over-clipped ratio ship as a regime discriminator?** Fact 683 measures it monotone in saturation extent (1.48x / 16.55x / 85.2x) and it predicts whether a stack's argmax flees or seeks clipped frames. It is cheap and it is the only diagnostic that distinguishes the two regimes. (M54, fact 683)

164. **Is there a detail metric that escapes single-frequency blindness?** Fact 708 shows the blindness is a property of evaluating at one frequency, not of lacking ground truth, so a spectral-*shape* statistic may escape it. M55 explicitly declined this as a design question rather than a verification one. **It is the live replacement candidate and nobody has attempted it.** (M55)
165. **Why is the flat-ROI contiguous penalty K-independent?** Fact 707: narrowing to K frames around focus recovers the detail ROI as the depth mechanism predicts, but 113's flat ROI does not move at all and two others go more negative. An unidentified mechanism operates on flat regions, and four of the six failing cells are flat. (M55, fact 707)
166. **What accounts for the 1.96e-02 against 2.6304e-01 discrepancy in A.0.2's self-reported defect?** M55's derivation is exact on the author's stated configuration, so the author likely measured a different ROI or quantity. Unresolved, and stated as a discrepancy rather than an error. (M55, fact 700)
167. **Should `b6_spec.py` gain monotonicity, homogeneity and negative-branch tests before anything else is built on it?** Fact 711: it is frozen, has eight golden vectors, and is blind to all three critical defects. Cheap, and it would have caught them. (M55, fact 711)
168. **Should code citations be machine-checked in CI?** Fact 713 measured 260 instances across 18 files, 11 of them defective. A checker that resolves the number is cheap and already implied; a checker that verifies the number is not stale, not prohibited, and not amended is the harder and more valuable half. (M56)
169. **Should a fact row carry a machine-readable DO-NOT-QUOTE flag?** Fact 715: the prohibition exists in prose in the row and was violated five times. A flag a linter can enforce is the only form that reaches the quoting site. (M56, fact 715)
170. **What is the citable form for a report-local number that folded as a QUESTION rather than a fact?** Fact 717: `stack.rs` prints "fact 200a" on every run, the claim is true, and no correct form exists to write instead. (M56, fact 717)
171. **Should every constant that traces to a fact carry a recomputing test in `calibrate.rs`'s shape?** Fact 720: exactly one green-but-false frozen-fixture test exists, and the crate already contains the pattern that would have caught it. (M56, fact 720)
172. **Does `read_noise_e_low`'s error at ISO 160 matter?** Fact 732: it is validated at ISO 50 and 100 and used at ISO 50 and 160, and the dark set has no ISO-160 row while the corpus has 22 real frames there. Needs the capture in section 11 of `captures-needed.md`. (M48)
173. **Where else is the corpus ISO distribution asserted?** M48 corrected two sites and explicitly did not sweep, because `merge.rs`, `focus.rs` and `stack.rs` belong to other agents. M48b's section-12 census is now the citation any such claim should carry. (M48, fact 727)
174. **Should the flat-ROI high-pass use `w = 3` rather than the shipped `w = 9`?** The sweep prefers it (structure contamination 1.0234x vs 1.2756x on `109MSDCF`), and it is a one-constant change with the evidence already logged in `experiments/m53-flat-ratio/out/a2_kernel_sweep.log`. It was not taken because **replacing an unmeasured choice with a different unmeasured choice repeats the error being fixed** (fact 757). What measurement decides it?
175. **Does anything in the corpus actually clip?** The censoring path is a bit-exact no-op on `109MSDCF` (fact 755) and has never been exercised where it fires. `foam` and `coffee-bubbles` are now known to be on disk (fact 756). **If neither clips, the censoring machinery has no end-to-end exercise anywhere and that is itself the finding.**
176. **The flat-ROI statistic is blind by construction to a very-low-spatial-frequency noise explosion**, because a 9x9 box high-pass cannot see below its cutoff. Documented, never tested. What is the cheapest plant that expresses this failure -- and does the LEGACY column, which is not high-passed, cover it?
177. **The new high-pass column has no baseline.** Fact 343's 1.0739x is a raw-sd number and cannot be carried across. Until a baseline is measured the column reports a ratio with nothing to compare it against, which is exactly the condition fact 469 criticised in its predecessor.
178. **What is the cheapest automated check that would have caught facts 694, 725, 759 and 763?** All four are instances of one missing invariant: nothing verifies a fold against its source, a row's internal consistency after amendment, or whether a cited number supports the sentence citing it. A row-level linter (cell count, count-vs-enumeration, citation resolves AND the cited row contains the claimed term) is a few hundred lines and would run in CI. **Fact 766 argues the absence is now the corpus's most reproducible defect.**
179. **Should the fold process require the source report's commit sha in the provenance cell?** Fact 765 shows an eleven-row fold whose source was never committed and is now unrecoverable. A sha in provenance makes the omission impossible to complete rather than merely discouraged -- the same move as fact 736's `FlatReference`.
180. **Is the `--stats` "fine noise sd" site in `merge_cmd.rs` a 5.94 site or a 1.815 site?** Fact 768 establishes that `through_focus.rs:514` needs 5.943 because both fields enter as the merge consumes them, while M53 independently concluded its own site needed 1.815 and repaired it that way. **Both agents measured; they may both be right about different sentences, which is exactly fact 768's point.** Nobody has compared the two sites side by side.
181. **Should the fact table carry, per row, the SEMANTIC configuration a number belongs to?** Facts 768, 770 and 772 are three instances of one failure: a number that is correct in one configuration quoted into another. A configuration tag would make the mis-pairing checkable by the linter question 178 proposes.
182. **Should the three-figure separation numbers still standing in facts 619 and 622 be re-quoted or deleted?** M55 states every separation number in this corpus quoted to three figures is over-precise and enumerates eleven. Facts 619 and 622 now carry a marker, but the digits are still there, inside rows whose headlines are already refuted. Marking is the cheap answer; deleting numbers from rows that no longer claim anything may be the correct one. (M58 omission 2, discipline 338)
183. **Facts 19 and 734 measure the same thing with the same statistic on the same stack and disagree by 27%.** Fact 19 -- the project's founding measurement -- reports naive argmax on `109MSDCF` at **1.253x** on the LEGACY raw-sd ratio. Fact 734 reports unregularised argmax on real `109MSDCF` frames at LEGACY **0.985x**. **Both are the legacy statistic on the same stack, and 0.985 is on the other side of 1.0 from 1.253.** The most likely explanation is that "unregularised argmax" names two different pipelines: M2's baseline was `ring(1,3)`, no alignment, pre-gain-fix, while today's carries Tenengrad (fact 70), a 27x27 window (fact 60) and the ISO-50 gain repair. **That is fact 768's class -- one label, two quantities -- applied to the project's own headline number, and it is ARGUED, not measured.** Until it is reconciled, `strehl` cannot honestly say the founding defect is removed, because the before-number and the after-number were not produced by the same instrument on the same configuration. Reconciling needs one run: today's binary, argmax with the regulariser off, M2's ROI and plane, both statistics reported. (Facts 19, 734; discipline 365)
184. **Is the 2.17x electron-domain step a conversion-gain change, and if so which mode is correctly calibrated?** A photon-transfer curve at ISO 500 or 1000 settles fact 790 **using data the corpus already has -- 261 of 421 frames are at those two settings -- and needs no new captures.** The ISO-160 PTC constrains only the low mode. **This is the highest-value follow-up in the corpus right now.** (M48c, facts 790-796)
185. **Is the shot-noise term above ISO 400 over-predicted by sqrt(2.17) ~ 1.47x in sigma?** It follows from fact 793 if the step is a conversion-gain change, and it would affect the majority of the corpus -- every ISO 500 and ISO 1000 stack. **It was NOT verified against illuminated data and does not follow from the dark measurement alone. Do not act on it before question 184.** (M48c, fact 793)
186. **What causes the 68x odd/even sensor-row asymmetry in the defect population?** Fact 788. It looks architectural rather than thermal, and if it is, a defect map for this body must carry row parity explicitly or be silently wrong under any flip or resample. (M48c, fact 788)
187. **Is a defect map worth building at all before darks at working exposure exist?** Facts 787, 801 and 808 together: 0.0414% of pixels, 99.8% isolated singletons, largest cluster 3 px, the merge will never remove them -- but the map is complete for the wrong exposure and misses an estimated 834 of 839 real-frame defects. **The agent's recommendation is one capture request rather than a module.** (M48c, fact 808)
